I keep mixing up simple things, which isn't like me. I'll get confused about appointment times, or... or the dates for the exhibits I help out with. I even forget which volunteer I've already called. It's just getting very hard to think straight right now.\n\nDoctor: Over what period of time have you noticed this fogginess and confusion developing — did it come on suddenly over days, or has it been gradually worsening over weeks or months?\n\nPatient: I'd say it's been going on for about four months now. It didn't just happen overnight. It really started back when we had some water damage at the house... which ended up pushing us into having to do a remodel. So, yes, it's been about four months of this.\n\nDoctor: Besides the confusion and fogginess, how has your mood been over these past four months? Have you been feeling sad, depressed, or down?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really felt sad or depressed. I mean, I get a little frustrated when I can't remember things, or when dealing with the house remodel gets to be a bit much... but I wouldn't say I feel down. My mood has actually been fine. It's really just the fogginess that's bothering me.\n\nDoctor: When you say your head feels foggy, are you having trouble actually remembering things that happened — like events from yesterday or last week — or is it more that you're having difficulty concentrating and processing information in the moment?\n\nPatient: Well, it's... I guess it's a bit of both, but mostly it's just this overall fogginess. I'm just mixing up simple things. Like I said, I'll get the appointment times wrong, or I'll confuse the dates for the exhibits at the museum where I volunteer. And I'll sit there and realize I can't remember which volunteer I just talked to on the phone. It's just... my thinking isn't clear, and I can't seem to keep those straight.\n\nDoctor: Besides the fogginess and trouble with concentration, have you noticed any other changes in how you're feeling physically — such as changes in your energy level, sleep, appetite, or weight over these four months?\n\nPatient: Well, as for my energy, I have noticed I'm just tired a lot. It's strange because even if I sleep well, I still wake up feeling tired. It's gotten to the point where by the time I just, you know, wash up in the morning and make myself a little breakfast, I already feel like I need to sit down again and rest. So my energy is definitely not what it used to be.\n\nDoctor: When you can't remember which volunteer you called, if someone told you their name, would you recognize it and remember the conversation, or is the memory itself gone?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I haven't really... tested it, I guess. Usually I'm just sitting there by myself trying to figure it out, so there's no one around to remind me of the name to see if it sparks anything. It just feels like a blank in the moment, and I couldn't tell you if the memory is completely gone or just hiding from me.\n\nDoctor: Does your fogginess and confusion change throughout the day — for example, is it worse at certain times, like in the morning or evening, or does it stay pretty much the same all day long?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed a difference, to be honest. It... it seems to stay pretty much the same all day long. I don't think it's worse in the morning or the evening, it's just kind of always there.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any fevers, headaches, or noticed any unusual smells or tastes recently? And have you been exposed to any chemicals, mold, or other substances during the house remodel?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any fevers or headaches. And I haven't noticed any strange smells or tastes, either. As for the remodel, no, I don't think I've been around any chemicals or mold or anything like that. It's just been regular construction stuff, you know, dust and paint, but nothing unusual.\n\nDoctor: Over these four months, would you say the fogginess and confusion have been getting progressively worse, or has it stayed about the same level since it started?\n\nPatient: I think it's stayed about the same. I don't feel like it's getting worse, it's just... it's just been a constant thing since it started. It hasn't really changed.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any recent infections, illnesses, or medical problems in the past four months — such as a urinary tract infection, pneumonia, or anything like that — even if it seemed minor at the time?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't been sick at all. Um... no infections or colds, or anything like a UTI. My health has been fine otherwise over these last few months, really. It's just been this confusion.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned you volunteer at the museum with the exhibits — are you still finding that work enjoyable, or has your interest in activities you used to enjoy changed over these past four months?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really been enjoying the museum lately. It's actually been like that with everything. Things I used to love, um... like my watercolors, my tomato plants, line dancing, or even just watching the cardinals out back... they all just sit there now. I just can't seem to make myself care about any of it anymore.\n\nDoctor: When you think about things you used to enjoy — like your watercolors or line dancing — do you find yourself feeling sad or down about not wanting to do them, or is it more that you just feel empty or numb about them?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I haven't thought about it quite like that. I don't know if I'd say I feel sad, or... or numb. It's just that the urge isn't there at all anymore. I guess I just haven't paid much attention to how I feel about not doing them, I just know I don't want to.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I keep mixing up simple things, which isn't like me. I'll get confused about appointment times, or... or the dates for the exhibits I help out with. I even forget which volunteer I've already called. It's just getting very hard to think straight right now.\n\nDoctor: Over what period of time have you noticed this fogginess and confusion developing — did it come on suddenly over days, or has it been gradually worsening over weeks or months?\n\nPatient: I'd say it's been going on for about four months now. It didn't just happen overnight. It really started back when we had some water damage at the house... which ended up pushing us into having to do a remodel. So, yes, it's been about four months of this.\n\nDoctor: Besides the confusion and fogginess, how has your mood been over these past four months? Have you been feeling sad, depressed, or down?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really felt sad or depressed. I mean, I get a little frustrated when I can't remember things, or when dealing with the house remodel gets to be a bit much... but I wouldn't say I feel down. My mood has actually been fine. It's really just the fogginess that's bothering me.\n\nDoctor: When you say your head feels foggy, are you having trouble actually remembering things that happened — like events from yesterday or last week — or is it more that you're having difficulty concentrating and processing information in the moment?\n\nPatient: Well, it's... I guess it's a bit of both, but mostly it's just this overall fogginess. I'm just mixing up simple things. Like I said, I'll get the appointment times wrong, or I'll confuse the dates for the exhibits at the museum where I volunteer. And I'll sit there and realize I can't remember which volunteer I just talked to on the phone. It's just... my thinking isn't clear, and I can't seem to keep those straight.\n\nDoctor: Besides the fogginess and trouble with concentration, have you noticed any other changes in how you're feeling physically — such as changes in your energy level, sleep, appetite, or weight over these four months?\n\nPatient: Well, as for my energy, I have noticed I'm just tired a lot. It's strange because even if I sleep well, I still wake up feeling tired. It's gotten to the point where by the time I just, you know, wash up in the morning and make myself a little breakfast, I already feel like I need to sit down again and rest. So my energy is definitely not what it used to be.\n\nDoctor: When you can't remember which volunteer you called, if someone told you their name, would you recognize it and remember the conversation, or is the memory itself gone?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I haven't really... tested it, I guess. Usually I'm just sitting there by myself trying to figure it out, so there's no one around to remind me of the name to see if it sparks anything. It just feels like a blank in the moment, and I couldn't tell you if the memory is completely gone or just hiding from me.\n\nDoctor: Does your fogginess and confusion change throughout the day — for example, is it worse at certain times, like in the morning or evening, or does it stay pretty much the same all day long?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed a difference, to be honest. It... it seems to stay pretty much the same all day long. I don't think it's worse in the morning or the evening, it's just kind of always there.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any fevers, headaches, or noticed any unusual smells or tastes recently? And have you been exposed to any chemicals, mold, or other substances during the house remodel?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any fevers or headaches. And I haven't noticed any strange smells or tastes, either. As for the remodel, no, I don't think I've been around any chemicals or mold or anything like that. It's just been regular construction stuff, you know, dust and paint, but nothing unusual.\n\nDoctor: Over these four months, would you say the fogginess and confusion have been getting progressively worse, or has it stayed about the same level since it started?\n\nPatient: I think it's stayed about the same. I don't feel like it's getting worse, it's just... it's just been a constant thing since it started. It hasn't really changed.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any recent infections, illnesses, or medical problems in the past four months — such as a urinary tract infection, pneumonia, or anything like that — even if it seemed minor at the time?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't been sick at all. It's actually been like that with everything. Things I used to love, um... like my watercolors, my tomato plants, line dancing, or even just watching the cardinals out back... they all just sit there now. I just can't seem to make myself care about any of it anymore.\n\nDoctor: When you think about things you used to enjoy — like your watercolors or line dancing — do you find yourself feeling sad or down about not wanting to do them, or is it more that you just feel empty or numb about them?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I guess I just haven't paid much attention to how I feel about not doing them, I just know I don't want to.\n\nDoctor: Over these past four months, even though you say your mood has been fine, have you been feeling persistently sad, down, or depressed most of the day, even on days when nothing particular is bothering you?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I haven't really felt sad or... or depressed at all. Like I said, my mood has been fine. I'm not sitting around crying or feeling down most of the day. It's really just what we talked about, with just not wanting to do my usual things. But no, I haven't been feeling sad.\n\nDoctor: Over these past four months, have you found yourself crying, or feeling like you might cry, more often than you normally would?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I don't think I've cried at all, really. Like I was just saying, I'm not feeling sad or anything like that. So no, no crying or feeling like I'm about to.\n\nDoctor: When the fogginess and confusion first started four months ago, did you already have that loss of interest in your hobbies and activities, or did the loss of interest in things like your watercolors and line dancing develop after the fogginess began?\n\nPatient: I'm honestly not too sure which came first. I can't say for certain if one started before the other.\n\nDoctor: When you think back over these four months, have you noticed that you feel less pleasure or satisfaction from things, even when you do manage to do them — or is it more that you just don't have the drive to start them in the first place?\n\nPatient: I think it's just that I can't make myself care about them anymore.
